In binary operations (and elsewhere) can we pass x and y through and put the unwrap as close to the cbegin call as possible? I don’t love the idea of passing x_data around across many function calls. You’ll still need to KEEP x_data, just want it to be very close to the cbegin call

# Making `r_wrap()` sound

This plan fixes a memory safety bug in our ALTREP wrapper (`src/wrapper.c`),
and makes sure the fix does not cost rray a copy of its inputs on every call.

The work lands as three stacked pull requests. Each one is green and does not
regress performance on its own.

1. `r_unwrap()`: add the complement to `r_wrap()`, and make every data read in
   `src/` go through it.

2. Lock the wrapper: port base R's fix, so a data pointer, once handed out,
   stays valid for as long as the wrapper is reachable.

3. Owned results: stop wrapping arrays that rray itself just allocated, in
   `rray_unstack()` and `rray_stack()`.

Read the whole document before starting. Part 1 explains the bug, Part 2 the
design, and Parts 3 to 5 list every change, pull request by pull request.

---

# Part 1: The bug

## How the wrapper works today

`r_wrap(x)` returns an ALTREP object whose `data1` slot holds the vector being
wrapped. rray uses it to change attributes (dimensions, names, titles) without
copying the data.

When something asks the wrapper for a data pointer, `src/wrapper.c` does this:

- Read-only request (`REAL_RO()`, `r_dbl_cbegin()`): return a pointer straight
  into `data1`.

- Writable request (`REAL()`, `r_dbl_begin()`): if `data1` might be shared,
  copy it, put the copy in `data1`, and return a pointer into the copy.

## What goes wrong

A read-only pointer taken before a writable request points into the old
`data1`, which the wrapper no longer holds.

```c
const double* p = REAL_RO(w);
double* q = REAL(w);
q[0] = 99;
// p[0] == 1, q[0] == 99, w[1] == 99
```

That pointer is stale at best. It can also dangle. Refcounts in R are only an
upper bound: they go down when a slot is overwritten, but not when the object
holding the reference is garbage collected. So the old `data1` can look shared
while nothing reachable holds it:

```r
x <- as.double(1:1e6)
l <- list(x)
w <- wrap(x)
rm(x, l)
```

After the swap, nothing reaches the old vector, and the next GC frees it. With
`MallocScribble=1` on macOS, reading `p[0]` after a GC returns `1.194531e+103`,
which is the `0x5555...` fill pattern of freed memory.

## Base R had the same bug

Base R's own wrapper class (`src/main/altclasses.c`) had exactly this pattern.
Ivan Krylov reported it, and Luke Tierney fixed it in three commits:

- `dbc99e13e8` (r90261, also in R-patched as r90260): the read-only `Dataptr`
  and `Dataptr_or_null` methods now copy shared data too, so a later writable
  request returns the same address.

- `c40bda6593` (r90309, R-devel): once any data pointer has been handed out,
  the wrapper is locked and its data is never replaced again. A shallow
  duplicate of a locked wrapper copies the data instead of sharing it.

- `3580627fa3` (r90315): handles `shallow_duplicate()` returning an object
  with a refcount above zero. This does not apply to us, because
  `r_clone_data()` always allocates a fresh vector.

---

# Part 2: Design

## The lock

The rule, taken from base R:

> No data pointer leaves the wrapper unless the wrapper is the only owner of
> its data. After that, the wrapper's data never changes.

Concretely:

- The first time any data pointer is requested (read-only or writable), the
  wrapper copies its data if the data is shared, then locks itself.

- A locked wrapper never replaces `data1`. Its data has a refcount of exactly
  1, because nothing in rray ever shares locked data (see the next two
  points).

- `r_wrap()` on a locked wrapper copies the data instead of sharing it.

- A shallow duplicate goes through `r_wrap()`, so it copies too.

The lock lives in `data2`, which we do not use today: `r_null` means unlocked,
`r_true` means locked.

`Dataptr_or_null` returns `NULL` for an unlocked wrapper and a pointer for a
locked one. It never copies and never locks. R falls back to `Elt` or
`Get_region` when it gets `NULL`, and we implement both. This differs from
base R, which copies here. `Dataptr_or_null` is meant to be cheap, and R calls
it often (for example in `ITERATE_BY_REGION`), so a copy there would make
plain reads expensive.

## The cost, and `r_unwrap()`

With the lock alone, every official read of a wrapper with shared data costs a
full copy. For rray that would be nearly every call, because `vec_as_array()`
(in `src/utils.c`) wraps every plain vector input, and the user's vector is
still bound in R:

```r
x <- as.double(1:1e6)
rray_sum(x)  # vec_as_array() wraps x, the sum reads it: full copy
```

So inside rray we never read data through the wrapper. We read it from the
vector underneath:

```c
r_obj* x_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(x));
const double* v_x = r_dbl_cbegin(x_data);
```

`r_unwrap(x)` returns `data1` for one of our wrappers and `x` for anything
else. It never copies and never locks.

Why this is safe:

- `x_data` is on our protect stack, so it stays alive even if something swaps
  the wrapper's `data1` while we read. The worst case is a stale view, and
  only if someone writes to `x` through the wrapper while we are reading.
  rray never writes to its inputs.

- `KEEP()` does not change refcounts, so borrowing never makes data look
  shared.

Outside rray, other packages and base R go through the official API. They pay
at most one copy per wrapper, and then get a pointer that stays valid.

## Rules for `x_data`

These hold everywhere in `src/`.

1. Name the unwrapped object `<name>_data`: `x_data`, `y_data`,
   `value_data`, `index_data`, `n_data`, `true_data`, and so on. Never
   `data`.

2. Always `KEEP()` the result of `r_unwrap()`, even when it looks reachable
   through `x`.

3. Read only data from `x_data`: `r_*_cbegin()`, `r_length()`,
   `r_typeof()`. Never read attributes from it. The wrapped vector still
   carries its own original attributes, which can be out of date. Dimensions
   and names always come from `x`.

4. Never let `x_data` reach an R object: never `r_list_poke()` it, never set
   it as an attribute, never return it to R. That would raise its refcount
   for good (refcounts do not go down when a list is garbage collected), and
   a locked wrapper's data must keep a refcount of 1. Passing `x_data`
   between C functions, or returning it from one C function to another, is
   fine as long as it stays protected.

5. Never write through `x_data`. Only `r_*_cbegin()` on it, never
   `r_*_begin()`.

## What gets unwrapped

Every `r_<type>_cbegin()` on an object that is often our own wrapper, or that
can be as long as an array input:

- Array payloads: `x`, `y`, `condition`, `true_`, `false_`, `missing`,
  `value`, the elements of `xs`.

- Subscripts and index arrays.

- `n` in `rray_roll_each()`, which goes through `rray_cast()` and
  `rray_broadcast()`.

Out of scope, with the reason:

- Dimensions, axes, and small integer arguments (`times` and `n` in
  `rray_rep()` and `rray_roll()`, `times` in `rray_rep_each()`, `dimensions`
  in `rray_split()`). These go through `arg_as_bare_integer()` and friends,
  which never wrap, so they are only wrappers if a user passes an rray result
  as the argument. Even then the cost is one copy of a short vector, after
  which that wrapper is locked and reads are free.

cat src/binary.h src/clone.c && sed -n 1,200p src/arithmetic-add.c && grep -n "rray_binary_arithmetic\b\|^r_obj\* rray_binary_arithmetic" -A60 src/arithmetic.c | head -120
#ifndef RRAY_BINARY_H
#define RRAY_BINARY_H

#include "rlang.h"

#include "strided-iterator.h"

#define RRAY_BINARY_ARGS(...) , __VA_ARGS__
#define RRAY_BINARY_NO_ARGS

#define RRAY_BINARY(                                                           \
  X_CTYPE,                                                                     \
  X_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
  X_CAST,                                                                      \
  Y_CTYPE,                                                                     \
  Y_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
  Y_CAST,                                                                      \
  OUT_RTYPE,                                                                   \
  OUT_CTYPE,                                                                   \
  OUT_DEREF,                                                                   \
  ONE,                                                                         \
  ONE_ARGS                                                                     \
)                                                                              \
  struct rray_run_iterator it;                                                 \
  rray_run_iterator_init2(                                                     \
    &it,                                                                       \
    v_dimensions,                                                              \
    dimensionality,                                                            \
    v_x_broadcast_strides,                                                     \
    v_y_broadcast_strides                                                      \
  );                                                                           \
                                                                               \
  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(OUT_RTYPE, rray_run_iterator_size(&it)));   \
  OUT_CTYPE* v_out = OUT_DEREF(out);                                           \
                                                                               \
  const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x);                                       \
  const Y_CTYPE* v_y = Y_CONST_DEREF(y);                                       \
                                                                               \
  for (; !rray_run_iterator_done(&it); rray_run_iterator_next2(&it)) {         \
    const r_ssize start = rray_run_iterator_start(&it);                        \
    const r_ssize end = rray_run_iterator_end(&it);                            \
                                                                               \
    r_ssize x_loc = rray_run_iterator_loc(&it, 0);                             \
    const r_ssize x_stride = rray_run_iterator_stride(&it, 0);                 \
                                                                               \
    r_ssize y_loc = rray_run_iterator_loc(&it, 1);                             \
    const r_ssize y_stride = rray_run_iterator_stride(&it, 1);                 \
                                                                               \
    if (x_stride == 0 && y_stride == 0) {                                      \
      const X_CTYPE x_elt = v_x[x_loc];                                        \
      const Y_CTYPE y_elt = v_y[y_loc];                                        \
      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
        v_out[i] = ONE(X_CAST(x_elt), Y_CAST(y_elt) ONE_ARGS);                 \
      }                                                                        \
    } else if (x_stride == 0) {                                                \
      const X_CTYPE x_elt = v_x[x_loc];                                        \
      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
        const Y_CTYPE y_elt = v_y[y_loc];                                      \
        v_out[i] = ONE(X_CAST(x_elt), Y_CAST(y_elt) ONE_ARGS);                 \
        y_loc += y_stride;                                                     \
      }                                                                        \
    } else if (y_stride == 0) {                                                \
      const Y_CTYPE y_elt = v_y[y_loc];                                        \
      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
        const X_CTYPE x_elt = v_x[x_loc];                                      \
        v_out[i] = ONE(X_CAST(x_elt), Y_CAST(y_elt) ONE_ARGS);                 \
        x_loc += x_stride;                                                     \
      }                                                                        \
    } else {                                                                   \
      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
        const X_CTYPE x_elt = v_x[x_loc];                                      \
        const Y_CTYPE y_elt = v_y[y_loc];                                      \
        v_out[i] = ONE(X_CAST(x_elt), Y_CAST(y_elt) ONE_ARGS);                 \
        x_loc += x_stride;                                                     \
        y_loc += y_stride;                                                     \
      }                                                                        \
    }                                                                          \
  }                                                                            \
                                                                               \
  FREE(1);                                                                     \
  return out;

#endif
#include "clone.h"

// Shallow duplicates only the data, not the attributes
r_obj* r_clone_data(r_obj* x) {
  switch (r_typeof(x)) {
  case R_TYPE_logical: {
    const int* v_x = r_lgl_cbegin(x);
    const r_ssize size = r_length(x);
    r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_logical(size));
    int* v_out = r_lgl_begin(out);
    r_memcpy(v_out, v_x, sizeof(int) * size);
    FREE(1);
    return out;
  }
  case R_TYPE_integer: {
    const int* v_x = r_int_cbegin(x);
    const r_ssize size = r_length(x);
    r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_integer(size));
    int* v_out = r_int_begin(out);
    r_memcpy(v_out, v_x, sizeof(int) * size);
    FREE(1);
    return out;
  }
  case R_TYPE_double: {
    const double* v_x = r_dbl_cbegin(x);
    const r_ssize size = r_length(x);
    r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_double(size));
    double* v_out = r_dbl_begin(out);
    r_memcpy(v_out, v_x, sizeof(double) * size);
    FREE(1);
    return out;
  }
  case R_TYPE_complex: {
    const r_complex* v_x = r_cpl_cbegin(x);
    const r_ssize size = r_length(x);
    r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_complex(size));
    r_complex* v_out = r_cpl_begin(out);
    r_memcpy(v_out, v_x, sizeof(r_complex) * size);
    FREE(1);
    return out;
  }
  case R_TYPE_raw: {
    const Rbyte* v_x = r_raw_cbegin(x);
    const r_ssize size = r_length(x);
    r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_raw(size));
    Rbyte* v_out = r_raw_begin(out);
    r_memcpy(v_out, v_x, sizeof(Rbyte) * size);
    FREE(1);
    return out;
  }
  case R_TYPE_character: {
    r_obj* const* v_x = r_chr_cbegin(x);
    const r_ssize size = r_length(x);
    r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_character(size));
    for (r_ssize i = 0; i < size; ++i) {
      r_chr_poke(out, i, v_x[i]);
    }
    FREE(1);
    return out;
  }
  case R_TYPE_list: {
    r_obj* const* v_x = r_list_cbegin(x);
    const r_ssize size = r_length(x);
    r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_list(size));
    for (r_ssize i = 0; i < size; ++i) {
      r_list_poke(out, i, v_x[i]);
    }
    FREE(1);
    return out;
  }
  default: {
    r_stop_unimplemented_type(r_typeof(x));
  }
  }
}

r_obj* ffi_rray_add(r_obj* ffi_x, r_obj* ffi_y, r_obj* ffi_frame) {
  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = ffi_frame, .env = r_null};
  return rray_add(ffi_x, ffi_y, rray_args.x, rray_args.y, error_call);
}

r_obj* rray_add(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* y,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* y_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  return rray_binary_arithmetic(
    x,
    y,
    rray_add_switch,
    x_arg,
    y_arg,
    error_call
  );
}

static rray_binary_arithmetic_fn rray_add_switch(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* y,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* y_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  const enum rray_type x_type = rray_typeof(x);

static r_obj* rray_sum_lgl(
  r_obj* x,
  bool na_rm,
  r_ssize out_size,
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* v_out_broadcast_strides,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  const int* v_x = r_lgl_cbegin(x);

  return rray_sum_lgl_or_int(
    v_x,
    r_globals.na_lgl,
    na_rm,
    out_size,
    v_dimensions,
    dimensionality,
    v_out_broadcast_strides,
    error_call
  );
}

static r_obj* rray_sum_int(
  r_obj* x,
  bool na_rm,
  r_ssize out_size,
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* v_out_broadcast_strides,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  const int* v_x = r_int_cbegin(x);
  const r_ssize x_size = r_length(x);
  const r_ssize count = rray_reduce_count(x_size, out_size);

  if (count > RRAY_SUM_INT64_MAX_COUNT) {
    return rray_sum_int_fallback(
      v_x,
      na_rm,
      out_size,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality,
      v_out_broadcast_strides,
      error_call
    );
  }

  return rray_sum_lgl_or_int(
    v_x,
    r_globals.na_int,
    na_rm,
    out_size,
    v_dimensions,
    dimensionality,
    v_out_broadcast_strides,
    error_call
  );
}

// Branchless in the atomic case for better performance in general. Not in the
// barrier case because the repeated `POKE`s are more expensive there.
#define RRAY_EXTRACT_ATOMIC_MASK_LOOP(POKE, MISSING)                           \
  do {                                                                         \
    const int* v_index = r_lgl_cbegin(subscript.index);                        \
    const r_ssize index_step = r_length(subscript.index) == 1 ? 0 : 1;         \
                                                                               \
    r_ssize i = 0;                                                             \
    r_ssize location = 0;                                                      \
                                                                               \
    while (i < subscript.size) {                                               \
      const int elt = v_index[location * index_step];                          \
      POKE(out, i, elt == r_globals.na_lgl ? MISSING : v_x[location]);         \
      i += elt != 0;                                                           \
      ++location;                                                              \
    }                                                                          \
  } while (0)

#define RRAY_EXTRACT_BARRIER_MASK_LOOP(POKE, MISSING)                          \
  do {                                                                         \
    const int* v_index = r_lgl_cbegin(subscript.index);                        \
    const r_ssize index_step = r_length(subscript.index) == 1 ? 0 : 1;         \
    const r_ssize x_size = r_length(x);                                        \
                                                                               \
    r_ssize i = 0;                                                             \
                                                                               \
    for (r_ssize location = 0; location < x_size; ++location) {                \
      const int elt = v_index[location * index_step];                          \
                                                                               \
      if (elt == 0) {                                                          \
        continue;                                                              \
      }                                                                        \
                                                                               \
      POKE(out, i, elt == r_globals.na_lgl ? MISSING : v_x[location]);         \
      ++i;                                                                     \
    }                                                                          \
  } while (0)

  } while (0)

#define RRAY_CAST(FROM_CTYPE, FROM_CBEGIN, TO_RTYPE, TO_CTYPE, TO_BEGIN, ONE)  \
  x = KEEP(vec_as_array(x));                                                   \
                                                                               \
  const r_ssize size = r_length(x);                                            \
  const FROM_CTYPE* v_x = FROM_CBEGIN(x);                                      \
                                                                               \
  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(TO_RTYPE, size));                           \
  TO_CTYPE* v_out = TO_BEGIN(out);                                             \
                                                                               \
  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < size; ++i) {                                         \
    v_out[i] = ONE(v_x[i]);                                                    \
  }                                                                            \
                                                                               \
  r_attrib_poke_dim(out, r_dim(x));                                            \
                                                                               \
  r_obj* names = r_dim_names(x);                                               \
                                                                               \
  if (names != r_null) {                                                       \
    r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, names);                                       \
  }                                                                            \
                                                                               \
  FREE(2);                                                                     \
  return out;
static r_obj* rray_roll_each_normalize(r_obj* n, int axis_dimension) {
  const r_ssize size = r_length(n);
  const int* v_n = r_int_cbegin(n);

  if (rray_roll_each_is_normalized(v_n, size, axis_dimension)) {
    return n;
  }

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_integer(size));
  int* v_out = r_int_begin(out);

  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < size; ++i) {
    v_out[i] = rray_roll_normalize(v_n[i], axis_dimension);
  }

  r_attrib_poke_dim(out, r_dim(n));

  FREE(1);
  return out;
}

static bool rray_roll_each_is_normalized(
  const int* v_n,
  r_ssize size,
  int axis_dimension
) {
  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < size; ++i) {
    if (rray_roll_normalize(v_n[i], axis_dimension) != v_n[i]) {
      return false;
    }
  }

  return true;
}
void check_roll_n_not_missing(
  r_obj* n,
  struct rray_arg* arg,

Edit plans/wrap.md 
-2. Always `KEEP()` the result of `r_unwrap()`, even when it looks reachable
-   through `x`.
-
-3. Read only data from `x_data`: `r_*_cbegin()`, `r_length()`,
-   `r_typeof()`. Never read attributes from it. The wrapped vector still
-   carries its own original attributes, which can be out of date. Dimensions
-   and names always come from `x`.
-
-4. Never let `x_data` reach an R object: never `r_list_poke()` it, never set
-   it as an attribute, never return it to R. That would raise its refcount
-   for good (refcounts do not go down when a list is garbage collected), and
-   a locked wrapper's data must keep a refcount of 1. Passing `x_data`
-   between C functions, or returning it from one C function to another, is
-   fine as long as it stays protected.
-
-5. Never write through `x_data`. Only `r_*_cbegin()` on it, never
-   `r_*_begin()`.
+2. Always `KEEP()` the result of `r_unwrap()`, even when it looks reachable
+   through `x`.
+
+3. Create `x_data` on the line right before the `r_*_cbegin()` that reads
+   it, and use it for nothing else. `r_length()`, `r_typeof()`, and every
+   attribute read stay on `x`. The wrapped vector still carries its own
+   original attributes, which can be out of date.
+
+4. `x_data` never leaves the function that creates it. Functions keep taking
+   and returning `x`. Never pass `x_data` to another function (the one
+   exception is `Rf_match()` in `rray_as_subscript_names()`), never return
+   it, never store it in a struct. In particular, never let it reach an R
+   object: no `r_list_poke()`, no attribute, no return to R. That would raise
+   its refcount for good (refcounts do not go down when a list is garbage
+   collected), and a locked wrapper's data must keep a refcount of 1.
+
+5. Never write through `x_data`. Only `r_*_cbegin()` on it, never
+   `r_*_begin()`.

Edit plans/wrap.md 
-Two patterns cover every site.
-
-Driver and kernel. A driver reads attributes from `x`, then hands `x` to a
-kernel that only reads data. The driver creates `x_data` right before the
-dispatch and passes it to the kernel. The kernel's parameter is renamed from
-`x` to `x_data`, along with its macro body and its prototype in
-`src/decl/*-decl.h`. The name then says what the kernel gets.
-
-Inline read. A function reads data from an object itself. It unwraps right
-before the read, in the same function.
-
-Subscripts are a special case of the inline pattern, covered in Part 3.
+At the `r_*_cbegin()` call, and nowhere else. Every site becomes:
+
+```c
+r_obj* x_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(x));
+const double* v_x = r_dbl_cbegin(x_data);
+```
+
+plus one more in the `FREE()` that ends the function or block. In a kernel
+macro, these two lines replace the `CONST_DEREF(x)` line, and the macro's
+`FREE()` goes up by one.
+
+So drivers, kernel signatures, typedefs, and `src/decl/*-decl.h` headers do
+not change. Drivers keep passing `x` and `y`, and only the kernel bodies
+change. A driver changes only where it calls `r_*_cbegin()` itself.
+
+Subscripts follow the same rule: `struct rray_subscript` keeps the original
+`index` in `.index`, and each reader unwraps it right before its own
+`r_*_cbegin()`.

## `src/clone.c`

`r_clone_data()` reads its input. It is called from `wrapper.c` (on data that
is never a wrapper) and from `rray_extract_assign()` and
`rray_slice_assign()` (on `x`, which is often a wrapper).

In each `case`, right before the `r_*_cbegin()`:

```c
case R_TYPE_double: {
  r_obj* x_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(x));
  const double* v_x = r_dbl_cbegin(x_data);
  const r_ssize size = r_length(x);
  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_double(size));
  double* v_out = r_dbl_begin(out);
  r_memcpy(v_out, v_x, sizeof(double) * size);
  FREE(2);
  return out;
}
```

`r_length(x)` stays on `x`. Every `FREE(1)` becomes `FREE(2)`. Include
`wrapper.h` from `clone.c`.

## Binary operations

File: `src/binary.h`.

`RRAY_BINARY()` is the only place the binary kernels read data. Replace

```c
const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x);
const Y_CTYPE* v_y = Y_CONST_DEREF(y);
```

with

```c
r_obj* x_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(x));
const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x_data);

r_obj* y_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(y));
const Y_CTYPE* v_y = Y_CONST_DEREF(y_data);
```

and change the final `FREE(1)` to `FREE(3)`. Include `wrapper.h` from
`binary.h`.

Nothing else changes. Every arithmetic, compare, equality, extremum, and
logical kernel goes through `RRAY_BINARY()`, and the drivers, typedefs, and
kernel signatures keep `x` and `y`.

## Cast

File: `src/cast.c`.

In `RRAY_CAST()` and `RRAY_CAST_LOSSY()`, replace
`const FROM_CTYPE* v_x = FROM_CBEGIN(x);` with

```c
r_obj* x_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(x));
const FROM_CTYPE* v_x = FROM_CBEGIN(x_data);
```

and change `FREE(2)` to `FREE(3)`. `r_length(x)`, `r_dim(x)`, and
`r_dim_names(x)` stay on `x`.

## Broadcast

File: `src/broadcast.c`.

In `RRAY_BROADCAST_ATOMIC()` and `RRAY_BROADCAST_BARRIER()`, replace the
`CONST_DEREF(x)` line with the unwrap and a read of `x_data`, and change
`FREE(1)` to `FREE(2)`.

`rray_broadcast()` and `rray_broadcast_common()` do not change.

## Combine and stack

File: `src/combine.c`.

In `RRAY_COMBINE_FILL_ATOMIC()` and `RRAY_COMBINE_FILL_BARRIER()`, replace
the `CONST_DEREF(x)` line with the unwrap and a read of `x_data`, and add
`FREE(1)` after the `RRAY_COMBINE_FILL_LOOP()`. These macros are `void`
function bodies with no protection of their own today.

`rray_combine()` and `rray_combine_fill()` do not change.

This is what fixes `rray_stack()`. `rray_stack_prepare()` wraps every input
with `rray_insert_axes()`, and those wrappers share the user's data. Today
`rray_combine()` reads them with `r_dbl_cbegin()` and friends. After Part 4,
that read would copy every input in full. With this change it reads the data
underneath, with no copy. `src/stack.c` itself needs no change for this.

## If else

File: `src/if-else.c`.

In `RRAY_IF_ELSE_FILL()`, the three reads at the top become

```c
r_obj* condition_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(condition));
const int* v_condition = r_lgl_cbegin(condition_data);

r_obj* true_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(true_));
CTYPE const* v_true = CBEGIN(true_data);

r_obj* false_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(false_));
CTYPE const* v_false = CBEGIN(false_data);
```

Inside the `missing != r_null` branch, the read becomes

```c
r_obj* missing_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(missing));
CTYPE const* v_missing = CBEGIN(missing_data);
```

with a `FREE(1)` at the end of that branch. The final `FREE(1)` becomes
`FREE(4)`.

`rray_if_else()` and the `rray_if_else_fill()` signature do not change.

## Index

File: `src/index.c`.

- `rray_index()`: the `v_v_index` loop unwraps each index array as it goes.
  The pointers outlive the loop, so every `index_data` stays protected until
  the end of the function:

  ```c
  int n_index_prot = 0;
  const int* v_v_index[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < indices_size; ++i) {
    r_obj* index_data = KEEP_N(r_unwrap(v_indices[i]), &n_index_prot);
    v_v_index[i] = r_int_cbegin(index_data);
  }
  ```

  Add `FREE(n_index_prot)` next to the final `FREE(5)`. The existing comment
  above the loop stays where it is.

- `rray_any_missing_index()`: in its loop, unwrap `index` right before
  `r_int_cbegin()`, read `index_data`, and `FREE(1)` at the end of each
  iteration, before the `break`. `r_length(index)` stays on `index`.

- `rray_as_index_array()`: replace `const int* v_x = r_int_cbegin(x);` with
  the unwrap and a read of `x_data`, and change `FREE(1)` to `FREE(2)`. It
  still returns `x`.

- In `RRAY_INDEX_ATOMIC()` and `RRAY_INDEX_BARRIER()`, replace the
  `CONST_DEREF(x)` line with the unwrap and a read of `x_data`, and change
  `FREE(1)` to `FREE(2)`.

## Subscripts

Files: `src/subscript.c`, `src/extract-subscript.c`,
`src/slice-subscript.c`.

`struct rray_subscript` keeps the original `index` in `.index`. Every
function that reads its data unwraps right before the read:

- `rray_as_subscript_complement()` and `rray_as_subscript_nonzero()`: in each
  `case`, unwrap `index` right before `r_int_cbegin()` or `r_dbl_cbegin()`,
  and add one to that path's `FREE()`. In `rray_as_subscript_complement()`,
  the `case` blocks end with `break`, so `FREE(1)` goes before each `break`.

- `rray_mask_size()`: unwrap `mask` before `r_lgl_cbegin()`, `FREE(1)`
  before `return`. `r_length(mask)` stays on `mask`.

- `rray_subscript_summarise()`: each `case` currently returns straight from
  the call. Unwrap at the top of the function, since both cases read, store
  the result in a local, then `FREE(1)` and return it:

  ```c
  r_obj* index_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(index));

  struct rray_subscript_summary out;

  switch (r_typeof(index)) {
  case R_TYPE_integer:
    out = rray_subscript_summarise_int(r_int_cbegin(index_data) + start, size);
    break;
  case R_TYPE_double:
    out = rray_subscript_summarise_dbl(r_dbl_cbegin(index_data) + start, size);
    break;
  default:
    r_stop_unreachable();
  }

  FREE(1);
  return out;
  ```

- `rray_as_subscript_names()`: unwrap right before `r_chr_cbegin()`, and pass
  `index_data` to `Rf_match()` too. `Rf_match()` is base R and may take a
  data pointer, which would lock the wrapper. Change `FREE(1)` to `FREE(2)`.

+`r_clone_data()` reads its input. It is called from `wrapper.c` (on data that
+is never a wrapper) and from `rray_extract_assign()` and
+`rray_slice_assign()` (on `x`, which is often a wrapper).
+
+In each `case`, right before the `r_*_cbegin()`:
+
+```c
+case R_TYPE_double: {
+  r_obj* x_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(x));
+  const double* v_x = r_dbl_cbegin(x_data);
+  const r_ssize size = r_length(x);
+  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_double(size));
+  double* v_out = r_dbl_begin(out);
+  r_memcpy(v_out, v_x, sizeof(double) * size);
+  FREE(2);
+  return out;
+}
+```
+
+`r_length(x)` stays on `x`. Every `FREE(1)` becomes `FREE(2)`. Include
+`wrapper.h` from `clone.c`.
+
+## Binary operations
+
+File: `src/binary.h`.
+
+`RRAY_BINARY()` is the only place the binary kernels read data. Replace
+
+```c
+const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x);
+const Y_CTYPE* v_y = Y_CONST_DEREF(y);
+```
+
+with
+
+```c
+r_obj* x_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(x));
+const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x_data);
+
+r_obj* y_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(y));
+const Y_CTYPE* v_y = Y_CONST_DEREF(y_data);
+```
+
+and change the final `FREE(1)` to `FREE(3)`. Include `wrapper.h` from
+`binary.h`.
+
+Nothing else changes. Every arithmetic, compare, equality, extremum, and
+logical kernel goes through `RRAY_BINARY()`, and the drivers, typedefs, and
+kernel signatures keep `x` and `y`.
+
+## Cast
+
+File: `src/cast.c`.
+
+In `RRAY_CAST()` and `RRAY_CAST_LOSSY()`, replace
+`const FROM_CTYPE* v_x = FROM_CBEGIN(x);` with
+
+```c
+r_obj* x_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(x));
+const FROM_CTYPE* v_x = FROM_CBEGIN(x_data);
+```
+
+and change `FREE(2)` to `FREE(3)`. `r_length(x)`, `r_dim(x)`, and
+`r_dim_names(x)` stay on `x`.
+
+## Broadcast
+
+File: `src/broadcast.c`.
+
+In `RRAY_BROADCAST_ATOMIC()` and `RRAY_BROADCAST_BARRIER()`, replace the
+`CONST_DEREF(x)` line with the unwrap and a read of `x_data`, and change
+`FREE(1)` to `FREE(2)`.
+
+`rray_broadcast()` and `rray_broadcast_common()` do not change.
+
+## Combine and stack
+
+File: `src/combine.c`.
+
+In `RRAY_COMBINE_FILL_ATOMIC()` and `RRAY_COMBINE_FILL_BARRIER()`, replace
+the `CONST_DEREF(x)` line with the unwrap and a read of `x_data`, and add
+`FREE(1)` after the `RRAY_COMBINE_FILL_LOOP()`. These macros are `void`
+function bodies with no protection of their own today.
+
+`rray_combine()` and `rray_combine_fill()` do not change.
+
+This is what fixes `rray_stack()`.

-`struct rray_subscript` carries a subscript from where it is checked to where
-it is read. The readers (the loops in `src/extract.c`,
-`src/extract-assign.c`, `rray_slice_as_locations()`) only ever read data from
-`.index`. So the constructors unwrap, and `.index` holds the unwrapped vector
-from then on. The readers need no change.
-
-The constructors still read attributes (`r_dim(index)`) from the original
-`index`, before unwrapping.
-
-- `rray_as_subscript_locations()`: unwrap first:
-  `r_obj* index_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(index));`. Use `index_data` for
-  `rray_subscript_summarise()`, `rray_as_subscript_complement()`,
-  `rray_as_subscript_nonzero()`, and `.index`. `FREE(1)` before each
-  `return`.
-
-- `rray_as_subscript_mask()`: same, using `index_data` for
-  `rray_mask_size()` and `.index`.
-
-- `rray_as_extract_mask()`: keep the `r_dim(index)` checks on `index`. In
-  the array path, unwrap after the dimensions check and use `index_data` for
-  `rray_mask_size()` and `.index`.
-
-- `rray_as_extract_points()`: keep `r_dim(index)` on `index`. Unwrap after
-  reading the dimensions and use `index_data` for
-  `rray_subscript_summarise()`, `r_typeof()`, and `.index`.
-
-- `rray_as_subscript_names()`: unwrap first and use `index_data` for
-  `r_chr_cbegin()` and for `Rf_match(names, index_data, 0)`.
-
-- `rray_subscript_summarise()`, `rray_mask_size()`,
-  `rray_as_subscript_complement()`, `rray_as_subscript_nonzero()`: no code
-  change, but their callers now always pass unwrapped data.
-
-- `rray_subscript_as_list()`: this is the only place a subscript goes back to
-  R, through the internal test helpers `rray_as_extract_subscript()` and
-  `rray_as_slice_subscript()`. Poke `r_clone_data(subscript.index)` instead
-  of `subscript.index`, so the unwrapped vector never reaches R (rule 4).
-  Poke it directly, the way the other two elements are poked.
-
-Every caller already does `KEEP(subscript.index)` (or `KEEP_N()`) right after
-building a subscript, so the unwrapped vector is protected from there on.
-Inside the constructors, nothing allocates between the final `FREE()` and the
-caller's `KEEP()`, which is the same pattern the code already relies on for
-the allocated cases.
-

The file ~/files/r/packages/rray/plans/wrap.md has been updated successfully.

I updated plans/wrap.md to work the way you asked. Functions now pass x and y through unchanged, and the unwrap sits on the line right before each cbegin(). I didn't commit anything.

The new rule (Part 2)

r_obj* x_data = KEEP(r_unwrap(x));
const double* v_x = r_dbl_cbegin(x_data);

  • x_data is only used by that cbegin(). r_length(), r_typeof() and all attribute reads stay on x.

  • x_data never leaves the function that creates it. It is never passed to another function, returned, or stored in a struct. The one exception is Rf_match() in rray_as_subscript_names(), because base R may take a data pointer there, which would lock the wrapper.

  • src/wrapper.c is exempt, since its own helpers pass the wrapped data between each other.

What this changes in Part 3

  • Binary operations: the whole change is now in RRAY_BINARY() in src/binary.h. It gets two unwraps and FREE(1) becomes FREE(3). All the parameter renames are gone: kernels, typedefs, decl headers, and the five drivers (whose FREE(5) no longer changes).

  • Other kernel macros (broadcast, index, extract, slice, permute, locate, reduce, cumulative, cast): the CONST_DEREF(x) line becomes the two lines above, and the macro's FREE() goes up by one. Drivers no longer change.

  • void fill macros (combine, split, roll each, extract assign, slice assign): these get a new KEEP() plus a FREE(1) after the loop, since they had no protection before.

  • Subscripts: the constructors no longer unwrap, and .index holds the original index. Each reader unwraps right before its read: the extract loop macros, rray_mask_size(), rray_subscript_summarise(), rray_as_subscript_complement(), rray_as_subscript_nonzero(), and rray_slice_as_locations(). Because the original is kept, the r_clone_data() workaround in rray_subscript_as_list() is gone.

  • rray_slice_as_locations() can still return subscript.index, which may be a wrapper. So rray_slice() and rray_slice_assign() now unwrap its result before r_int_cbegin().

  • r_clone_data(): each case unwraps itself. The r_clone_data_impl() split and the new decl header are dropped.

  • Index: rray_any_missing_index() now unwraps inside its own loop, so moving the v_v_index loop earlier is no longer needed.

  • Hand-written reduce kernels (rray_sum_lgl() and friends): these currently return straight from a helper call. They now store the result in out, then FREE(1) and return it.

Protection

x_data stays protected for as long as its pointer is used. Where the pointer outlives a loop (v_v_index in rray_index(), v_v_locations in slice), the plan keeps x_data protected with KEEP_N() until the function's final FREE(). The Part 6 check list now names the early returns to watch: RRAY_LOCATE(), rray_roll_each_normalize(), and the reduce kernels.

Parts 4 and 5 didn't need changes.

r_obj* rray_expand_dimensionality(
  r_obj* x,
  int dimensionality,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, arg, error_call);
  x = KEEP(arg_as_array(x, arg, error_call));

  r_obj* x_dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions(x, arg, error_call));
  const int* v_x_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(x_dimensions);
  const int x_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);

  if (dimensionality <= x_dimensionality) {
    r_stop_internal(
      "`dimensionality` (%d) must be greater than the dimensionality of %s "
      "(%d).",
      dimensionality,
      rray_arg_format(arg),
      x_dimensionality
    );
  }

  check_dimensionality(dimensionality);

  r_obj* out_dimensions = KEEP(r_alloc_integer(dimensionality));
  int* v_out_dimensions = r_int_begin(out_dimensions);
  r_memcpy(v_out_dimensions, v_x_dimensions, sizeof(int) * x_dimensionality);

  for (int i = x_dimensionality; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
    v_out_dimensions[i] = 1;
  }

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_wrap(x));
  r_attrib_poke_dim(out, out_dimensions);

  r_obj* x_names = r_dim_names(x);

  if (x_names != r_null) {
    r_obj* out_names = KEEP(r_alloc_list(dimensionality));
    r_obj* const* v_x_names = r_list_cbegin(x_names);

    for (int i = 0; i < x_dimensionality; ++i) {
      r_list_poke(out_names, i, v_x_names[i]);
    }

    r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, out_names);
    FREE(1);
  }

r_wrap() saves one full copy of the data, at the moment rray changes an object's attributes. That's all it saves. Whether the copy is saved for good, or only put off, depends on what reads the result next.

Where it's used

1. vec_as_array() in src/utils.c. A plain vector with no dim gets wrapped and given a 1D dim. Arrays pass through untouched. Every function that takes an array argument goes through this (arithmetic, compare, reduce, cumulative, broadcast, slice, extract, if-else, and so on). So the saving only applies when the input is a plain vector:

x <- as.double(1:1e6)
rray_sum(x)            # saves a copy of x
rray_sum(array(x))     # no wrap, nothing to save

2. The attribute setters. These return the wrapper to the user:

  • rray_set_dimensions()

  • rray_set_names()

  • rray_set_axis_names(), rray_set_row_names(), rray_set_column_names()

  • rray_set_titles(), rray_set_axis_title()

  • rray_insert_axes(), rray_remove_axes()

Base R's dim(x) <- d on a shared vector copies the data. These cost O(1) instead.

3. Inside rray, as temporary objects. rray_stack() wraps each input through rray_insert_axes() and rray_expand_dimensionality(), then rray_combine() copies everything into the output. The wrap saves one copy per input (two copies become one). rray_unstack() and rray_stack() also wrap arrays they just allocated, which saves nothing. That is Part 5 of the plan.

How much it really buys after the lock

  • Read inside rray: the copy is saved for good. With r_unwrap(), rray reads the data underneath directly, which covers all of case 1 and 3. So rray_set_dimensions(x, d) |> rray_sum() never copies.

  • Read by base R or another package through a data pointer (for example +, sum(), and most C code): the first read copies once and locks the wrapper. You end up paying the same copy base R would have, just later, plus an ALTREP lookup on later element access.

  • Never read, or the data wasn't shared to begin with: no copy ever.

My take

The real win is case 1: plain vectors going into any rray function, with no copy. It touches every function, and with r_unwrap() it costs nothing extra. The setters only win while the result stays inside rray. Once it reaches base R, it's back to base R's cost.

Case 1 could also be done without a wrapper. Every place that needs dimensions could treat "no dim" as length(x) and never build a dimensioned object. I haven't checked how many places need an actual dimensioned object, such as rray_broadcast() returning its input as is. If that number is small, the wrapper would only be needed for the setters, which is a much smaller surface to keep memory safe.

I can benchmark one of these, for example rray_sum() on a plain 1e6 vector with and without the wrap, to see whether the saved copy matters next to the work itself.

#include "dimensions.h"

#include "dimensionality.h"
#include "size.h"
#include "utils.h"
#include "wrapper.h"

#include "decl/dimensions-decl.h"

r_obj* ffi_rray_dimensions(r_obj* ffi_x, r_obj* ffi_frame) {
  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = ffi_frame, .env = r_null};
  return rray_dimensions(ffi_x, rray_args.x, error_call);
}

r_obj* rray_dimensions(
  r_obj* x,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, arg, error_call);
  x = KEEP(arg_as_array(x, arg, error_call));
  r_obj* out = r_dim(x);
  FREE(1);
  return out;
}

int rray_dimension(
  r_obj* x,
  int axis,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  r_obj* dimensions = rray_dimensions(x, arg, error_call);
  return r_int_get(dimensions, axis - 1);
}

bool rray_dimensions_are_equal(
  const int* v_x_dimensions,
  int x_dimensionality,
  const int* v_y_dimensions,
  int y_dimensionality
) {
  if (x_dimensionality != y_dimensionality) {
    return false;
  }

  for (int i = 0; i < x_dimensionality; ++i) {
    if (v_x_dimensions[i] != v_y_dimensions[i]) {
      return false;
    }
  }

  return true;
}

  FREE(1);
  return out;
}

void check_unclassed(r_obj* x, struct rray_arg* arg, struct r_lazy error_call) {
  if (r_is_object(x)) {
    r_abort_lazy_call(
      error_call,
      "%s must be a bare array, not %s.",
      rray_arg_format_input(arg),
      r_obj_type_friendly(x)
    );
  }
}

r_obj* arg_as_array(r_obj* x, struct rray_arg* arg, struct r_lazy error_call) {
  if (rray_typeof(x) == RRAY_TYPE_scalar) {
    stop_scalar_input(x, arg, error_call);
  }

  return vec_as_array(x);
}

static inline int rray_dimension2(
  int x,
  int y,
  int axis,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* y_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  if (x == y) {
    return x;
  }

  if (x == 1) {
    return y;
  }

  if (y == 1) {
    return x;
  }

  stop_incompatible_dimensions(x, y, axis, x_arg, y_arg, error_call);

static r_obj* arg_as_integer(
  r_obj* x,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  switch (r_typeof(x)) {
  case R_TYPE_integer:
    return x;
  case R_TYPE_double:
    return arg_as_integer_from_double(x, arg, error_call);

    if (elt < 0) {
      r_abort_lazy_call(
        error_call,
        "%s must not contain negative values.",
        rray_arg_format(arg)
      );
    }
  }

  FREE(1);
  return x;
}
r_obj* rray_titles(r_obj* x, struct rray_arg* arg, struct r_lazy error_call) {
  r_obj* names = KEEP(rray_names(x, arg, error_call));
  r_obj* out = names == r_null ? r_null : r_names(names);
  FREE(1);
  return out;
}

  r_obj* out = KEEP(rray_set_titles(x, new_titles, arg, error_call));

  FREE(3);
  return out;
}

static r_obj* rray_clear_titles(r_obj* x) {
  r_obj* names = r_dim_names(x);

  if (names == r_null || r_names(names) == r_null) {
    return x;
  }

  r_obj* new_names = KEEP(r_clone(names));
  r_attrib_poke_names(new_names, r_null);

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_wrap(x));
  r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, new_names);

  FREE(2);
  return out;
}

static r_obj* rray_clear_axis_title(
  r_obj* x,
  int axis,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  r_obj* names = r_dim_names(x);
  r_obj* old_titles = names == r_null ? r_null : r_names(names);

  if (old_titles == r_null) {
    return x;
  }

Yes, I think that works, and it's better than wrapping. Once nothing inside rray needs a dimensioned object, vec_as_array() doesn't need to wrap. Most of the r_unwrap() plan then goes away, because rray functions mostly see plain vectors and arrays, not wrappers.

What it takes

Low-level helpers that treat a plain vector as 1D:

  • rray_dimensions(x): returns dim(x), or length(x) as an integer when there's no dim. It already exists, but it currently calls arg_as_array() and then r_dim().

  • A dimnames helper: returns dimnames(x), or list(names(x)) for a vector. Titles come along with it, since rray_titles() reads the names of the dimnames list.

  • arg_as_array() becomes only the scalar and class check, and returns x unchanged.

The protection catch. r_dim() never allocates, so many of today's ~75 r_dim(), r_dim_names() and r_names() calls (across about 35 files) don't protect the result. The new helpers allocate in the vector case, so every call site needs a KEEP(). That's the main mechanical cost, and it all goes through the same protection pass.

Places where a plain vector would leak out where an array is expected. I grepped for functions that return their input as is:

  • rray_broadcast() returns x when the dimensions already match. A plain vector would come back without dim. That path needs to build a real 1D array (copy, or wrap).

  • rray_cast() returns vec_as_array(x) when the type already matches. All its callers (rray_cast_common(), if-else, extract assign, slice assign, roll each) only read dimensions and data. So once they use the helpers, it can return x itself. The lossy and widening casts allocate their output and set dim from rray_dimensions(x), which is fine.

  • rray_as_index_array() returns x, and its result only gets read internally, so it's the same story.

I didn't find any other function that returns its input. Everything else allocates its output.

What's left for r_wrap()

After this, r_wrap() is only used by the user-facing setters: rray_set_dimensions(), the names and titles setters, rray_insert_axes() and rray_remove_axes(). It's also used internally by rray_stack(), which goes through rray_insert_axes(), rray_expand_dimensionality() and rray_set_axis_names().

As I said earlier, for the setters the wrapper mostly puts the copy off. It only avoids the copy when the result goes straight back into rray. So you now have a real choice:

  1. Keep the wrapper for the setters. Parts 2 to 5 of the plan stay, but r_unwrap() reads only need to handle a setter's result being passed into rray.

  2. Drop the wrapper entirely. The setters copy and set attributes, like base R's dim<- does on shared data. src/wrapper.c and the whole memory-safety problem go away. For rray_stack(), the inputs would be expanded without copies through the helpers instead of rray_insert_axes().

I'd lean toward option 2. Once the helpers exist, the wrapper's only benefit is a deferred copy in setter chains, and it costs a lot of tricky code to keep memory safe.

Do you want me to rewrite plans/wrap.md around the helpers, and if so, with or without the wrapper for the setters?

Davis

Do a full rewrite of the plan without the wrapper

Start by isolating which of the key functions need to natively handle 1D vectors

Make those into a checklist we can do 1 PR at a time.

Then make another checklist of functions to transition off usage of vec_as_array. Instead we will have check_atomic() after check_unclassed() to take its place. Each function will need careful analysis to also change off r_dim() and the like

If you think a function can return a 1D vector back to the user, call that out especially. I’m not sure if we want this yet. It sort of makes sense for rray functions to always return an array even if they allow a 1D vector

Claude

// Operations such as `*`, `/`, and `^` are either more efficient or more
// correct (around infinities) if they go through the C99 `_Complex`. The C99
// standard guarantees that `_Complex` has the same representation as a two
// element array of its real type, so we can `memcpy()` it over. Analysis of
// the assembly shows that this copy disappears entirely, so it's not a
// performance hit.
static inline double _Complex rray_cpl_to_c99(r_complex x) {
  double _Complex out;
  memcpy(&out, &x, sizeof(out));
  return out;
}
static inline r_complex rray_c99_to_cpl(double _Complex x) {
  r_complex out;
  memcpy(&out, &x, sizeof(out));
  return out;
}
// In tight loops, the compiler can often vectorize a bitwise `|` better than
// `||`. Writing `f(x) | g(y)` directly on two `bool` calls triggers clang's
// `-Wbitwise-instead-of-logical`, so we route it through this helper instead.
static inline bool bool_bitwise_or(bool x, bool y) {
  return (int) x | (int) y;
}
static inline bool bool_bitwise_and(bool x, bool y) {
  return (int) x & (int) y;
}

r_obj* ffi_rray_set_dimensions(
  r_obj* ffi_x,
  r_obj* ffi_dimensions,
  r_obj* ffi_frame
) {
  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = ffi_frame, .env = r_null};
  return rray_set_dimensions(ffi_x, ffi_dimensions, rray_args.x, error_call);
}

r_obj* rray_set_dimensions(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* dimensions,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, arg, error_call);
  x = KEEP(arg_as_array(x, arg, error_call));
  dimensions =
    KEEP(arg_as_dimensions(dimensions, rray_args.dimensions, error_call));

  const r_ssize x_size = rray_size(x, arg, error_call);

  const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions);
  const int* v_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(dimensions);

  const r_ssize size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);

  if (x_size != size) {
    r_abort_lazy_call(
      error_call,
      "Can't set these dimensions. "
      "Can't change from a size of %" R_PRI_SSIZE " to a size of %" R_PRI_SSIZE
      ".",
      x_size,
      size
    );
  }

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_wrap(x));

  r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, r_null);
  r_attrib_poke_dim(out, dimensions);

  FREE(3);
  return out;
}

r_obj* ffi_rray_dimensions_common(
  r_obj* ffi_xs,
  r_obj* ffi_dimensions,
  r_obj* ffi_frame
) {
  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = ffi_frame, .env = r_null};
  return rray_dimensions_common(
    ffi_xs,
    ffi_dimensions,
    rray_args.empty,
    error_call
  );
}

r_obj* rray_dimensions_common(
  r_obj* xs,
  r_obj* dimensions,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  if (dimensions != r_null) {
    return arg_as_dimensions(dimensions, rray_args.dot_dimensions, error_call);
  }

  return rray_dimensions_common_opts(xs, NULL, 0, arg, error_call);
}

r_obj* rray_dimensions_common_opts(
  r_obj* xs,
  const int* v_ignore,
  r_ssize ignore_size,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  const r_ssize n = r_length(xs);

  if (n == 0) {
    r_abort_lazy_call(error_call, "Must supply at least one array to `...`.");
  }

  r_obj* const* v_xs = r_list_cbegin(xs);
  r_obj* xs_names = KEEP(r_names(xs));

  r_ssize x_i = 0;
  struct rray_arg* x_arg = new_subscript_arg(arg, xs_names, n, &x_i);
  KEEP(x_arg->shelter);

  r_ssize out_i = 0;
  struct rray_arg* out_arg = new_subscript_arg(arg, xs_names, n, &out_i);
  KEEP(out_arg->shelter);

  int out_dimensionality = 1;

  // Stack allocated array of known max size that we accumulate the common
  // dimensions in. Initialized to 1, which works very nicely with broadcasting.
  int v_out_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];

  // Index of the input that set each axis of `v_out_dimensions`, always
  // populated by the time that axis can conflict
  r_ssize v_out_args[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];

  for (int i = 0; i < RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY; ++i) {
    v_out_dimensions[i] = 1;
    v_out_args[i] = 0;
  }

  bool v_ignored[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_memset(v_ignored, 0, sizeof(bool) * RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY);

  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < ignore_size; ++i) {
    v_ignored[v_ignore[i] - 1] = true;
  }

  for (; x_i < n; ++x_i) {
    r_obj* x = v_xs[x_i];

    r_obj* x_dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions(x, x_arg, error_call));
    const int* v_x_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(x_dimensions);
    const int x_dimensionality =
      rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);
    check_dimensionality(x_dimensionality);

    // Update `v_out_dimensions` and `out_dimensionality` in place
    // with common dimensions
    rray_dimensions_merge(
      v_out_dimensions,
      v_out_args,
      v_ignored,
      &out_dimensionality,
      &out_i,
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      x_i,
      out_arg,
      x_arg,
      error_call
    );

    FREE(1);
  }

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_integer(out_dimensionality));
  int* v_out = r_int_begin(out);
  r_memcpy(v_out, v_out_dimensions, sizeof(int) * out_dimensionality);

  FREE(4);
  return out;
}

static inline void rray_dimensions_merge(
  int* v_out_dimensions,
  r_ssize* v_out_args,
  const bool* v_ignored,
  int* p_out_dimensionality,
  r_ssize* p_out_i,
  const int* v_x_dimensions,
  int x_dimensionality,
  r_ssize x_i,
  struct rray_arg* out_arg,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  const int out_dimensionality = *p_out_dimensionality;

  const int common_dimensionality = (out_dimensionality > x_dimensionality)
    ? out_dimensionality
    : x_dimensionality;

  *p_out_dimensionality = common_dimensionality;

  for (int i = 0; i < common_dimensionality; ++i) {
    if (v_ignored[i]) {
      continue;
    }

    const int out_dimension =
      (i < out_dimensionality) ? v_out_dimensions[i] : 1;
    const int x_dimension = (i < x_dimensionality) ? v_x_dimensions[i] : 1;

    *p_out_i = v_out_args[i];

    const int dimension = rray_dimension2(
      out_dimension,
      x_dimension,
      i + 1,
      out_arg,
      x_arg,
      error_call
    );

    if (dimension != out_dimension) {
      v_out_dimensions[i] = dimension;
      v_out_args[i] = x_i;

111 more lines cut

  r_obj* x,

  FREE(1);

  return rray_set_names(ffi_x, ffi_names, rray_args.x, error_call);
}

r_obj* rray_set_names(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* names,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, arg, error_call);
  x = KEEP(arg_as_array(x, arg, error_call));

  if (names != r_null) {
    if (r_typeof(names) != R_TYPE_list) {
      r_abort_lazy_call(
        error_call,
        "%s must be a list or `NULL`, not %s.",
        rray_arg_format(rray_args.names),
        r_obj_type_friendly(names)
      );
    }

    r_obj* dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions(x, arg, error_call));
    const int* v_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(dimensions);
    const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions);

    if (r_length(names) != dimensionality) {
      r_abort_lazy_call(
        error_call,
        "%s must have length %d to match the dimensionality of %s, "
        "not length %" R_PRI_SSIZE ".",
        rray_arg_format(rray_args.names),
        dimensionality,
        rray_arg_format(arg),
        r_length(names)
      );
    }

    r_obj* const* v_names = r_list_cbegin(names);
    for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
      check_axis_names(v_names[i], i + 1, v_dimensions[i], error_call);
    }

    FREE(1);
  }

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_wrap(x));
  r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, names);

  FREE(2);
  return out;
}

r_obj* ffi_rray_set_axis_names(
  r_obj* ffi_x,
  r_obj* ffi_axis,
  r_obj* ffi_names,
  r_obj* ffi_frame
) {
  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = ffi_frame, .env = r_null};
  const int axis = arg_as_int(ffi_axis, rray_args.axis, error_call);
  return rray_set_axis_names(ffi_x, axis, ffi_names, rray_args.x, error_call);
}

) {

  r_obj* ffi_x,
  r_obj* ffi_names,
  r_obj* ffi_frame
) {

  return rray_set_axis_names(ffi_x, 2, ffi_names, rray_args.x, error_call);

r_obj* rray_set_axis_names(
  r_obj* x,
  int axis,
  r_obj* names,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, arg, error_call);
  x = KEEP(arg_as_array(x, arg, error_call));

  const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality(x, arg, error_call);
  check_axis(axis, dimensionality, rray_args.axis, error_call);

  const int dimension = rray_dimension(x, axis, arg, error_call);
  check_axis_names(names, axis, dimension, error_call);

  r_obj* old_names = KEEP(rray_names(x, arg, error_call));

  r_obj* new_names = KEEP(r_alloc_list(dimensionality));
  if (old_names != r_null) {
    r_obj* const* v_old_names = r_list_cbegin(old_names);
    for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
      r_list_poke(new_names, i, v_old_names[i]);
    }
  }
  r_list_poke(new_names, axis - 1, names);

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_wrap(x));
  r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, new_names);

  FREE(4);
  return out;
}

static inline void check_axis_names(
  r_obj* names,
  int axis,
  int dimension,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  if (names == r_null) {
    return;
  }

  if (r_typeof(names) != R_TYPE_character) {
    r_abort_lazy_call(
      error_call,
      "Names for axis %d must be a character vector or `NULL`, not %s.",
      axis,
      r_obj_type_friendly(names)
    );
  }

  if (r_length(names) != dimension) {
    r_abort_lazy_call(
      error_call,
      "Names for axis %d must have length %d, not length %" R_PRI_SSIZE ".",
      axis,
      dimension,
      r_length(names)
    );
  }
}

static const bool V_IGNORED_FALSE[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY] = {false};

}

r_obj* rray_broadcast_names2(r_obj* x, r_obj* y, r_obj* dimensions) {
  r_obj* out =
    KEEP(rray_broadcast_names_fill(r_null, x, dimensions, V_IGNORED_FALSE));
  out = rray_broadcast_names_fill(out, y, dimensions, V_IGNORED_FALSE);
  FREE(1);
  return out;
}

r_obj* ffi_rray_broadcast_names_common(r_obj* ffi_xs, r_obj* ffi_dimensions) {
  return rray_broadcast_names_common(ffi_xs, ffi_dimensions);
}

r_obj* rray_broadcast_names_common(r_obj* xs, r_obj* dimensions) {
  return rray_broadcast_names_common_opts(xs, dimensions, NULL, 0);
}

r_obj* rray_broadcast_names_common_opts(
  r_obj* xs,
  r_obj* dimensions,
  const int* v_ignore,
  r_ssize ignore_size
) {
  const r_ssize n = r_length(xs);
  r_obj* const* v_xs = r_list_cbegin(xs);

  bool v_ignored[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_memset(v_ignored, 0, sizeof(bool) * RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY);

  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < ignore_size; ++i) {
    v_ignored[v_ignore[i] - 1] = true;
  }

  r_obj* out = r_null;
  r_keep_loc out_loc;
  KEEP_HERE(out, &out_loc);

  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    out = rray_broadcast_names_fill(out, v_xs[i], dimensions, v_ignored);
    KEEP_AT(out, out_loc);
  }

  FREE(1);
  return out;
}

// Broadcasting of array names
//
// Assumes the inputs are validated arrays and are broadcastable to the
// `dimensions`. The caller typically checks all of this already.
static r_obj* rray_broadcast_names_fill(
  r_obj* out,
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* dimensions,
  const bool* v_ignored
) {
  r_obj* x_names = rray_names(x, rray_args.x, r_lazy_null);
  if (x_names == r_null) {
    return out;
  }
  KEEP(x_names);

  r_obj* const* v_x_names = r_list_cbegin(x_names);

  r_obj* x_dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions(x, rray_args.x, r_lazy_null));
  const int* v_x_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(x_dimensions);
  const int x_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);

  const int* v_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(dimensions);
  const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions);

  r_keep_loc out_loc;
  KEEP_HERE(out, &out_loc);

  for (int i = 0; i < x_dimensionality; ++i) {
    if (v_ignored[i]) {
      continue;
    }
    if (v_x_names[i] == r_null) {
      // No names to contribute on this axis
      continue;
    }
    if (v_x_dimensions[i] != v_dimensions[i]) {
      // No names to contribute when axis is broadcast
      continue;
    }
    if (out != r_null && r_list_get(out, i) != r_null) {
      // Names already exist on this axis
      continue;
    }
    if (out == r_null) {
      out = r_alloc_list(dimensionality);
      KEEP_AT(out, out_loc);
    }
    r_list_poke(out, i, v_x_names[i]);
  }

  FREE(3);
  return out;
}



r_obj* ffi_rray_reduce_names(r_obj* ffi_x, r_obj* ffi_axes) {
  return rray_reduce_names(ffi_x, ffi_axes);
}

// Reduction of array names
//
// Assumes the inputs are validated arrays and that `axes` are valid axes of
// `x`. The caller typically checks all of this already.
//
// Mimics `rray_broadcast_names()` ideas, but reduction only ever has 1 input
r_obj* rray_reduce_names(r_obj* x, r_obj* axes) {
  r_obj* x_names = rray_names(x, rray_args.x, r_lazy_null);
  if (x_names == r_null) {
    return r_null;
  }
  KEEP(x_names);

  r_obj* const* v_x_names = r_list_cbegin(x_names);

  r_obj* x_dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions(x, rray_args.x, r_lazy_null));
  const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);

  const int* v_axes = r_int_cbegin(axes);
  const r_ssize axes_size = r_length(axes);

  r_obj* out = r_null;
  r_keep_loc out_loc;
  KEEP_HERE(out, &out_loc);

  for (int i = 0; i < dimensionality; ++i) {
    if (v_x_names[i] == r_null) {
      // No names to contribute on this axis
      continue;
    }
    if (axis_is_reduced(i, v_axes, axes_size)) {
      // No names to contribute when axis is reduced
      continue;
    }
    if (out == r_null) {
      out = r_alloc_list(dimensionality);
      KEEP_AT(out, out_loc);
    }
    r_list_poke(out, i, v_x_names[i]);
  }

  FREE(3);
  return out;
}

static inline bool axis_is_reduced(
  int axis,
  const int* v_axes,
  r_ssize axes_size
) {
  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < axes_size; ++i) {
    if (v_axes[i] - 1 == axis) {
      return true;
    }
  }

  return false;
}


r_obj* ffi_rray_cast_common(r_obj* ffi_xs, r_obj* ffi_to, r_obj* ffi_frame) {
  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = rray_syms.dot_call, .env = ffi_frame};

  struct r_lazy arg_lazy = {.x = rray_syms.dot_arg, .env = ffi_frame};
  struct rray_arg arg = new_lazy_arg(&arg_lazy);

  struct r_lazy to_arg_lazy = {.x = rray_syms.dot_to_arg, .env = ffi_frame};
  struct rray_arg to_arg = new_lazy_arg(&to_arg_lazy);

  return rray_cast_common(ffi_xs, ffi_to, &arg, &to_arg, error_call);
}

  r_obj* xs,
  r_obj* to,

r_no_return void stop_unsupported_arithmetic(
  const char* op,
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* y,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* y_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {

    error_call,
    "Can't apply `%s` to %s and %s.",
    op,
    rray_arg_type_format(x_arg, rray_typeof(x)),
    rray_arg_type_format(y_arg, rray_typeof(y))
  );

Bash: sed -n 21,146p src/broadcast.c; sed -n 284,310p src/broadcast.c 
sed -n 21,146p src/broadcast.c; sed -n 284,310p src/broadcast.c
r_obj* rray_broadcast(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* dimensions,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, arg, error_call);
  x = KEEP(arg_as_array(x, arg, error_call));

  dimensions =
    KEEP(arg_as_dimensions(dimensions, rray_args.dimensions, error_call));

  r_obj* x_dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions(x, arg, error_call));

  const int* v_x_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(x_dimensions);
  const int* v_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(dimensions);

  const int x_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);
  const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions);
  check_dimensionality(dimensionality);

  if (
    rray_dimensions_are_equal(
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality
    )
  ) {
    FREE(3);
    return x;
  }

  check_broadcastable(
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    v_dimensions,
    dimensionality,
    arg,
    error_call
  );

  r_ssize v_x_broadcast_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  rray_fill_broadcast_strides_from_dimensions(
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    dimensionality,
    v_x_broadcast_strides
  );

  r_obj* out;

  switch (r_typeof(x)) {
  case R_TYPE_logical:
    out = rray_broadcast_lgl(
      x,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality,
      v_x_broadcast_strides
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_integer:
    out = rray_broadcast_int(
      x,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality,
      v_x_broadcast_strides
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_double:
    out = rray_broadcast_dbl(
      x,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality,
      v_x_broadcast_strides
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_complex:
    out = rray_broadcast_cpl(
      x,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality,
      v_x_broadcast_strides
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_raw:
    out = rray_broadcast_raw(
      x,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality,
      v_x_broadcast_strides
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_character:
    out = rray_broadcast_chr(
      x,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality,
      v_x_broadcast_strides
    );
    break;
  case R_TYPE_list:
    out = rray_broadcast_list(
      x,
      v_dimensions,
      dimensionality,
      v_x_broadcast_strides
    );
    break;
  default:
    r_stop_unreachable();
  }

  KEEP(out);
  r_attrib_poke_dim(out, dimensions);

  r_obj* out_names = KEEP(rray_broadcast_names(x, dimensions));

  if (out_names != r_null) {
    r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, out_names);
  }

  FREE(5);
  return out;
}

r_obj* rray_broadcast_common(
  r_obj* xs,
  r_obj* dimensions,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  dimensions =
    KEEP(rray_dimensions_common(xs, dimensions, rray_args.empty, error_call));

  const r_ssize n = r_length(xs);
  r_obj* const* v_xs = r_list_cbegin(xs);
  r_obj* xs_names = KEEP(r_names(xs));

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_list(n));
  r_attrib_poke_names(out, xs_names);

  r_ssize i = 0;
  struct rray_arg* x_arg = new_subscript_arg(NULL, xs_names, n, &i);
  KEEP(x_arg->shelter);

  for (; i < n; ++i) {
    r_list_poke(out, i, rray_broadcast(v_xs[i], dimensions, x_arg, error_call));
  }

  FREE(4);
  return out;
}

sed -n 21,121p src/cast.c; sed -n 31,150p src/combine.c
r_obj* rray_cast(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* to,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* to_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, x_arg, error_call);
  check_unclassed(to, to_arg, error_call);

  const enum rray_type x_type = rray_typeof(x);
  const enum rray_type to_type = rray_typeof(to);

  switch (x_type) {
  case RRAY_TYPE_logical:
    switch (to_type) {
    case RRAY_TYPE_logical:
      return vec_as_array(x);
    case RRAY_TYPE_integer:
      return rray_cast_lgl_to_int(x, x_arg, error_call);
    case RRAY_TYPE_double:
      return rray_cast_lgl_to_dbl(x, x_arg, error_call);
    case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
      return rray_cast_lgl_to_cpl(x, x_arg, error_call);
    case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
      stop_scalar_input(to, to_arg, error_call);
    default:
      stop_incompatible_cast(x_type, to_type, x_arg, error_call);
    }
  case RRAY_TYPE_integer:
    switch (to_type) {
    case RRAY_TYPE_logical:
      return rray_cast_int_to_lgl(x, x_arg, error_call);
    case RRAY_TYPE_integer:
      return vec_as_array(x);
    case RRAY_TYPE_double:
      return rray_cast_int_to_dbl(x, x_arg, error_call);
    case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
      return rray_cast_int_to_cpl(x, x_arg, error_call);
    case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
      stop_scalar_input(to, to_arg, error_call);
    default:
      stop_incompatible_cast(x_type, to_type, x_arg, error_call);
    }
  case RRAY_TYPE_double:
    switch (to_type) {
    case RRAY_TYPE_logical:
      return rray_cast_dbl_to_lgl(x, x_arg, error_call);
    case RRAY_TYPE_integer:
      return rray_cast_dbl_to_int(x, x_arg, error_call);
    case RRAY_TYPE_double:
      return vec_as_array(x);
    case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
      return rray_cast_dbl_to_cpl(x, x_arg, error_call);
    case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
      stop_scalar_input(to, to_arg, error_call);
    default:
      stop_incompatible_cast(x_type, to_type, x_arg, error_call);
    }
  case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
    switch (to_type) {
    case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
      return vec_as_array(x);
    case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
      stop_scalar_input(to, to_arg, error_call);
    default:
      stop_incompatible_cast(x_type, to_type, x_arg, error_call);
    }
  case RRAY_TYPE_character:
    switch (to_type) {
    case RRAY_TYPE_character:
      return vec_as_array(x);
    case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
      stop_scalar_input(to, to_arg, error_call);
    default:
      stop_incompatible_cast(x_type, to_type, x_arg, error_call);
    }
  case RRAY_TYPE_raw:
    switch (to_type) {
    case RRAY_TYPE_raw:
      return vec_as_array(x);
    case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
      stop_scalar_input(to, to_arg, error_call);
    default:
      stop_incompatible_cast(x_type, to_type, x_arg, error_call);
    }
  case RRAY_TYPE_list:
    switch (to_type) {
    case RRAY_TYPE_list:
      return vec_as_array(x);
    case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
      stop_scalar_input(to, to_arg, error_call);
    default:
      stop_incompatible_cast(x_type, to_type, x_arg, error_call);
    }
  case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
    stop_scalar_input(x, x_arg, error_call);
  }

  r_stop_unreachable();
}
r_obj* rray_combine(
  r_obj* xs,
  int axis,
  r_obj* ptype,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct rray_arg* ptype_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  if (r_length(xs) == 0) {
    r_abort_lazy_call(error_call, "Must supply at least one array to `...`.");
  }

  ptype = KEEP(rray_ptype_common(xs, ptype, arg, ptype_arg, error_call));

  xs = KEEP(rray_cast_common(xs, ptype, arg, ptype_arg, error_call));

  const r_ssize xs_size = r_length(xs);
  r_obj* const* v_xs = r_list_cbegin(xs);

  const int out_dimensionality = list_max_dimensionality(xs, arg, error_call);
  check_dimensionality(out_dimensionality);

  check_axis(axis, out_dimensionality, rray_args.dot_axis, error_call);

  r_obj* out_dimensions =
    KEEP(rray_dimensions_common_opts(xs, &axis, 1, arg, error_call));
  int* v_out_dimensions = r_int_begin(out_dimensions);

  int axis_dimension = 0;

  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < xs_size; ++i) {
    r_obj* x = v_xs[i];
    r_obj* x_dimensions = r_dim(x);
    const int x_dimensionality =
      rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);
    const int x_axis_dimension =
      (axis <= x_dimensionality) ? r_int_get(x_dimensions, axis - 1) : 1;

    if (axis_dimension > INT_MAX - x_axis_dimension) {
      r_abort_lazy_call(
        error_call,
        "The combined dimension on `.axis` is too large."
      );
    }

    axis_dimension += x_axis_dimension;
  }

  v_out_dimensions[axis - 1] = axis_dimension;

  const r_ssize out_size = rray_size_from_dimensions_checked(
    v_out_dimensions,
    out_dimensionality,
    error_call
  );

  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(r_typeof(ptype), out_size));

  r_ssize v_out_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  rray_fill_strides_from_dimensions(
    v_out_dimensions,
    out_dimensionality,
    v_out_strides
  );

  int v_x_broadcast_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_memcpy(
    v_x_broadcast_dimensions,
    v_out_dimensions,
    sizeof(int) * out_dimensionality
  );

  r_ssize v_x_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];

  r_ssize axis_offset = 0;

  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < xs_size; ++i) {
    r_obj* x = v_xs[i];
    r_obj* x_dimensions = r_dim(x);
    const int* v_x_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(x_dimensions);
    const int x_dimensionality =
      rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);
    const int x_axis_dimension =
      (axis <= x_dimensionality) ? v_x_dimensions[axis - 1] : 1;

    v_x_broadcast_dimensions[axis - 1] = x_axis_dimension;

    rray_fill_broadcast_strides_from_dimensions(
      v_x_dimensions,
      x_dimensionality,
      out_dimensionality,
      v_x_strides
    );

    const r_ssize out_start = axis_offset * v_out_strides[axis - 1];

    rray_combine_fill(
      x,
      out,

static r_obj* rray_combine_names(
  r_obj* xs,
  r_obj* dimensions,
  int axis,
  int axis_dimension
) {
  r_obj* out = rray_broadcast_names_common_opts(xs, dimensions, &axis, 1);
  r_keep_loc out_loc;
  KEEP_HERE(out, &out_loc);

  r_obj* axis_names = KEEP(rray_combine_axis_names(xs, axis, axis_dimension));

  if (axis_names != r_null) {
    if (out == r_null) {
      out = r_alloc_list(rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions));
      KEEP_AT(out, out_loc);
    }
    r_list_poke(out, axis - 1, axis_names);
  }

  FREE(2);
  return out;
}

static r_obj* rray_combine_axis_names(r_obj* xs, int axis, int axis_dimension) {
  const r_ssize n = r_length(xs);
  r_obj* const* v_xs = r_list_cbegin(xs);

  r_obj* out = r_null;
  r_keep_loc out_loc;
  KEEP_HERE(out, &out_loc);

  r_ssize out_i = 0;

  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    r_obj* x = v_xs[i];
    r_obj* dimensions = r_dim(x);
    const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions);
    const int dimension =
      (axis <= dimensionality) ? r_int_get(dimensions, axis - 1) : 1;

    r_obj* names = r_dim_names(x);
    r_obj* axis_names = (names != r_null && axis <= dimensionality)
      ? r_list_get(names, axis - 1)
      : r_null;

    if (axis_names != r_null) {
      if (out == r_null) {
        out = r_alloc_character(axis_dimension);
        KEEP_AT(out, out_loc);
      }

      r_obj* const* v_axis_names = r_chr_cbegin(axis_names);

      for (int j = 0; j < dimension; ++j) {
        r_chr_poke(out, out_i + j, v_axis_names[j]);
      }
    }

    out_i += dimension;
  }

  FREE(1);
  return out;
