Write tests/testthat/helper-wrapper-mutations.R 
mutation_vector <- function(type) {
  switch(
    type,
    logical = rep(c(TRUE, FALSE, NA, TRUE), 25L),
    integer = 1:100 + 0L,
    double = as.double(1:100) + 0.5,
    complex = complex(real = 1:100, imaginary = 100:1),
    raw = as.raw(1:100),
    character = paste0("s", 1:100),
    list = as.list(1:100 + 0L)
  )
}

mutation_cases <- function(type) {
  cases <- switch(
    type,
    logical = mutation_cases_logical(),
    integer = mutation_cases_numeric(),
    double = mutation_cases_numeric(),
    complex = mutation_cases_complex(),
    raw = mutation_cases_raw(),
    character = mutation_cases_character(),
    list = mutation_cases_list()
  )

  cases <- c(cases, mutation_cases_any())

  if (type != "raw") {
    cases <- c(cases, mutation_cases_missing())
  }

  cases
}

expect_no_mutation <- function(type) {
  cases <- mutation_cases(type)

  for (name in names(cases)) {
    f <- cases[[name]]

    x <- mutation_vector(type)
    x_before <- unserialize(serialize(x, NULL))

    w <- rray_set_dimensions(x, c(10L, 10L))
    plain <- unserialize(serialize(w, NULL))

    expect_identical(f(w), f(plain), info = name)
    expect_identical(x, x_before, info = name)
    expect_identical(w, plain, info = name)
    expect_identical(
      obj_address(wrapper_data(w)),
      obj_address(x),
      info = name
    )
  }
}

mutation_cases_any <- function() {
  list(
    print = function(w) utils::capture.output(print(w)),
    format = function(w) format(w),
    str = function(w) utils::capture.output(utils::str(w)),
    summary = function(w) summary(w),
    head = function(w) utils::head(w),
    identical = function(w) identical(w, w),
    all_equal = function(w) all.equal(w, w),
    dput = function(w) utils::capture.output(dput(w)),
    serialize = function(w) unserialize(serialize(w, NULL)),
    rds = function(w) {
      path <- tempfile()
      on.exit(unlink(path))
      saveRDS(w, path)
      readRDS(path)
    },
    subset = function(w) w[1:5, 2],
    subset2 = function(w) w[[3]],
    split = function(w) split(w, rep(1:2, 50L)),
    match = function(w) match(w[1:3], w),
    in_op = function(w) w[1:3] %in% w,
    rev = function(w) rev(w),
    as_list = function(w) as.list(w),
    lapply = function(w) lapply(1:3, function(i) w[[i]]),
    do_call_c = function(w) do.call(c, list(w, w)),
    assign_elt = function(w) {
      y <- w
      y[1] <- y[2]
      y
    },
    assign_elt2 = function(w) {
      y <- w
      y[[2]] <- y[[3]]
      y
    },
    assign_all = function(w) {
      y <- w
      y[] <- y[1]
      y
    },
    assign_dim = function(w) {
      y <- w
      dim(y) <- c(20L, 5L)
      y
    },
    assign_dim_null = function(w) {
      y <- w
      dim(y) <- NULL
      y
    },
    assign_dimnames = function(w) {
      y <- w
      dimnames(y) <- list(letters[1:10], LETTERS[1:10])
      y
    },
    assign_attr = function(w) {
      y <- w
      attr(y, "foo") <- 1
      y
    },
    assign_class = function(w) {
      y <- w
      class(y) <- "foo"
      unclass(y)
    },
    assign_diag = function(w) {
      y <- w
      diag(y) <- y[1]
      y
    },
    single_binding = function(w) {
      local({
        y <- rray_set_dimensions(w, 100L)
        y[1] <- y[2]
        y
      })
    },
    rray = function(w) {
      rray_slice(rray_reverse(w, axes = 1L), 1:3, 1:2)
    }
  )
}

mutation_cases_missing <- function() {
  list(
    assign_is_na = function(w) {
      y <- w
      is.na(y) <- 3
      y
    }
  )
}

The immutable wrapper is implemented and pushed to fix/wrap as two commits:

  • eaaad1f: the rewritten plan

  • 20bc81f: the implementation

The full test suite passes (7,076 tests) and devtools::check() is clean. I didn't have any questions along the way.

What changed

  • src/wrapper.c: implemented as the plan describes.

    • r_wrap() copies ALTREP inputs into a plain vector and marks every wrapper not mutable.
    • data1 is never replaced.
    • Every pointer request returns a pointer into data1 without copying.
    • Duplicate returns a plain vector.
    • Set_elt aborts.

  • test-wrapper.R: the ownership tests now cover the new behavior:

    • pointer stability for all seven types
    • a changed wrapper becoming a plain vector, with the second change happening in place
    • a temporary wrapper result (rray_set_dimensions(x, d) + 1) leaving x unchanged
    • ALTREP inputs being copied when wrapped
    • a snapshot of the Set_elt errors

  • test-wrapper-mutations.R: the battery, with its cases in helper-wrapper-mutations.R. It uses the real wrapper, about 230 operations across the seven types, and 1,604 expectations. Each case checks that the wrapper gives the same result as a plain array, that the original vector and the wrapper are unchanged, and that the wrapper still shares the original's memory.

Proof the battery catches a broken wrapper

I removed MARK_NOT_MUTABLE() and ran the wrapper tests:

  • Battery: all seven type tests failed.

    • For the five atomic types, assigning into a single-binding result wrote straight into the user's original vector.
    • For character and list, the same assignment hit the aborting Set_elt.

  • test-wrapper.R: the "changing a wrapper gives a plain vector" tests failed too.

The temporary-result test (rray_set_dimensions(x, d) + 1) did not fail in that run, so it's only a defensive check right now. The single-binding battery case is what actually catches the reuse.

Stale comments for you to update

Per AGENTS.md, I left these as they are, although they now describe the old behavior:

  • r_wrap() header: "Writable access forces a shallow duplication of the wrapped object if it EVER looks shared, ..."

  • In r_wrap(): "We manage writable access to the underlying data by always cloning it before giving out a writable handle if it is ever MAYBE_SHARED() ..."

  • In r_wrap(): "Reference count on data is bumped when we wrap it here. It now looks at least "referenced", but not necessarily "shared"." The refcount of data no longer matters.

  • In wrapper_duplicate(): "Wrapper only guarantees shallow ownership."

The two comments attached to code I deleted (inside wrapper_writable(), and on the shallow branch of wrapper_duplicate()) went with that code.

CI will run the battery across macOS, Windows, and Linux release, devel, and oldrel-1 to oldrel-3 once a PR is open. I haven't opened one.

Davis

How do you feel about this idea of cloning ALTREP on the way in? Is it overkill?

Do we basically already have the problem since 1:n will allocate in REAL_RO? So it’s not really fixing anything?

Claude

No, I don't think it's overkill, but the plan gives the wrong reason for it. You're right that the allocation argument is weak. The real reason is pointer stability.

The allocation argument mostly doesn't hold

Calling INTEGER_RO() on a plain 1:n already allocates: the compact sequence expands itself on its first data pointer request. Any C code reading 1:n carries that risk today, wrapper or not.

So copying at wrap time doesn't remove an allocation from the world; it moves it from our Dataptr method into r_wrap(). That gives our class a clean "methods never allocate" guarantee. In practice, the code reading a wrapped 1:n is exposed to roughly the same risk as code reading a plain 1:n. If that were the only reason, I'd call it overkill.

The real reason: a foreign ALTREP decides whether our pointers stay valid

Rule 1 says pointers into data1 stay valid for as long as the wrapper is alive. That only holds if data1's own pointer is stable. With a plain vector it always is. With another ALTREP class, it's only as stable as that class makes it.

The worrying case is base R's own wrapper. It has the exact bug we just fixed in every released R; the fix (r90261 and r90309) is only in R-devel. And base R creates these wrappers constantly: dim<-, dimnames<-, names<- and attr<- on a shared vector of 64 or more elements return a base R wrapper instead of copying (duplicate_attr(), since R 3.6). So this is a very likely rray input:

x <- as.double(1:100)
y <- x
dim(y) <- c(10L, 10L)            # y is now a base R wrapper around x
w <- rray_set_dimensions(y, 100L)

Without the copy, w's data1 would be y. Here's how that goes wrong:

  1. Our wrapper hands out a pointer obtained through INTEGER_RO(y).

  2. Any C code later calls REAL(y) on y. Lots of code does that just to read.

  3. In released R, base R's wrapper then copies its data and swaps it in, exactly the bug we fixed.

  4. Our earlier pointer now points into the old data. If nothing else holds that old data, it dangles.

The copy is what keeps rule 1 true no matter what other ALTREP classes do. Other packages' classes (arrow, vroom, memory-mapped vectors) are probably fine, but whether their pointers stay put is their contract, not something we can guarantee.

Narrower policies I considered and rejected

  • Copy only when DATAPTR_OR_NULL() returns NULL. This lets an unexpanded 1:n through without copying. But in released R, base R's wrapper returns a pointer there, so it would slip through, and it's the one case that matters.

  • Expand 1:n in place instead of copying it. This means detecting a compact sequence, which needs R's class objects, and those aren't part of the API. It's not worth it.

One improvement worth making

r_clone_data() reads an ALTREP through r_*_cbegin(). For 1:n, that first expands the sequence and caches the expansion on the user's object, and then copies it. That's two allocations of size n.

Copying ALTREP inputs with *_GET_REGION() instead fills our plain vector straight from the sequence, so it's one allocation of size n. That's the same cost as reading 1:n directly. Character and list have no region method, so they'd stay as they are.

So my suggestion is:

  • keep the copy

  • rewrite the plan's rationale around pointer stability (base R's wrapper is the concrete case), not allocation

  • optionally, make the copy use GET_REGION, plus a test that wrap(1:1e6) leaves 1:1e6 unexpanded

Want me to do any of those?

Oct 16, 2026, 21:49 UTC

Davis

I mean, I feel like we shouldn’t make decisions based on base R’s bug which is now fixed in future R versions. And I feel like we should expect other ALTREP classes to uphold a stable pointer invariant

Claude

That's fair on both counts. Base R's bug is fixed going forward, and until then every dim<- user is exposed to it, so it isn't rray's to work around. A stable data pointer is the basic contract of Dataptr, so it's reasonable to rely on other classes keeping it. And without the copy, the allocation point we just discussed comes out the same as reading 1:n directly.

So I'll drop the copy. The wrapper then wraps any input as is, and its own methods never allocate; only the wrapped class's Dataptr might, as it would anyway. I'll also add ALTREP inputs to the battery, so foreign data1 gets the same coverage.

Read plans/wrap.md 
50	
51	## The rules
52	
53	1. **`data1` never changes.** It is set once in `r_wrap()`. Every pointer
54	   into it stays valid for as long as the wrapper is reachable, because the
55	   wrapper keeps `data1` alive.
56	
57	2. **`data1` is a plain vector, never ALTREP.** `r_wrap()` copies an ALTREP
58	   input into a plain vector, unless it is one of our wrappers, whose `data1`
59	   is already plain. So no method ever has to ask another ALTREP class for a
60	   pointer.
61	
62	3. **Every wrapper is marked not mutable**, with `MARK_NOT_MUTABLE()`. R then
63	   treats the wrapper as shared, so it duplicates the wrapper before changing
64	   it in place.
65	
66	4. **`Duplicate` always returns a plain vector**, never a wrapper. So every
67	   change R makes lands on a fresh vector R owns.
68	
69	5. **A data pointer request never copies.** A read-only request returns a
70	   pointer into `data1`. A writable request on an atomic wrapper also returns
71	   a pointer into `data1`. A writable request on a character or list wrapper
72	   aborts, as it does today.
73	
74	6. **`Set_elt` aborts.** Only code that writes into an object it does not
75	   own can reach it.
76	
77	## Why rule 5 returns a pointer for writable requests
78	
79	A lot of C code asks for a writable pointer only to read. `REAL()`,
80	`INTEGER()` and friends are writable requests, and in R 4.6 even `RAW_RO()`
81	calls `RAW()` internally (see the note in `src/rlang/vec.h`). In testing,
82	about 600 base R operations made 30,303 writable requests on wrappers. All
83	of them were reads. Aborting there would break base R.
84	
85	## Why this is safe
86	
87	`MARK_NOT_MUTABLE()` sets the refcount to its maximum, and
88	`DECREMENT_REFCNT()` never lowers it from there (`src/include/Defn.h`). Every
89	place where R reuses an object's memory, or writes into an argument instead
90	of allocating, first checks `NO_REFERENCES()`, `MAYBE_REFERENCED()` or
91	`MAYBE_SHARED()`. That is 72 checks across `arithmetic.c`, `subassign.c`,
92	`coerce.c`, `attrib.c`, `eval.c` and others. All of them see the wrapper as
93	shared, and copy first.
94	
95	The case that matters most is reuse of a temporary. `w + 1` writes the result
96	into `w`'s memory when `w` has no references (`R_allocOrReuseVector()` in
97	`src/main/arithmetic.h`). That is how a temporary rray result gets a writable
98	request today. A not-mutable wrapper never passes that check.
99	
100	A prototype of this design was tested against R 4.6:
101	
102	- About 600 base R operations on wrappers of all seven types, sharing data
103	  with a vector still bound in R: 831 wrappers, 30,303 writable requests, no
104	  change to any wrapper's data or to any original vector. Character and list
105	  wrappers got no writable requests and no `Set_elt` calls.
106	
107	- rray's own test suite: 4,310 wrappers, no change to any wrapper's data.
108	  The only writable requests from rray itself were raw reads, through the
109	  `RAW_RO()` issue above.
110	
111	Part 4 turns this into a permanent test file, run on every CI platform.
112	
113	## What rule 2 and rule 5 buy
114	
115	No method of the wrapper allocates, apart from `Duplicate`, which R expects
116	to allocate. Dataptr, Dataptr_or_null, Elt, Get_region and Length only read
117	`data1`, a plain vector. So `REAL_RO()` and friends on a wrapper never
118	allocate and never longjmp.
119	
120	## Costs
121	
122	- **The first change to a wrapper copies**, even when nothing else shares its
123	  data. `y <- rray_set_dimensions(x, d); y[1] <- 0` copies `y` once, and
124	  later changes to `y` happen in place. Base R's `dim<-` would usually have
125	  copied at the reshape instead, so overall this is no worse than base R.
126	
127	- **ALTREP inputs are copied when wrapped.** `rray_set_dimensions(1:100, d)`
128	  copies the compact sequence into a plain vector. Reading a compact
129	  sequence's data would have expanded it anyway.
130	
131	- **Code that breaks R's rules writes through to the original.** C code that
132	  writes into an object through `REAL()` without checking whether it is
133	  shared would write into the user's original vector. That is wrong values,
134	  not unsafe memory, and the same code already does it to any ordinary shared
135	  vector.
136	
137	---
138	
139	# Part 3: Changes to `src/wrapper.c`
140	
141	Keep the file ordered top down per AGENTS.md: `r_wrap()`, then the class,
142	data, generic, dataptr, element, and test helper sections, as today.
143	
144	## `r_wrap()`
145	
146	```c
147	r_obj* r_wrap(r_obj* x) {
148	  R_altrep_class_t cls = wrapper_class(r_typeof(x));
149	
150	  r_obj* data = KEEP(wrapper_data_from(x));
151	
152	  r_obj* out = KEEP(R_new_altrep(cls, data, r_null));
153	
154	  r_attrib_clone_from(out, x);
155	
156	  MARK_NOT_MUTABLE(out);
157	
158	  FREE(2);
159	  return out;
160	}
161	```
162	
163	`data` needs the `KEEP()` now, because `wrapper_data_from()` can allocate and
164	`R_new_altrep()` allocates after it.
165	
166	## Data section
167	
168	Replace `wrapper_readonly()` and `wrapper_writable()` with:
169

171	static inline r_obj* wrapper_data(r_obj* x) {
172	  return R_altrep_data1(x);
173	}
174	
175	static r_obj* wrapper_data_from(r_obj* x) {
176	  if (is_wrapper(x)) {
177	    return wrapper_data(x);
178	  }
179	
180	  if (ALTREP(x)) {
181	    return r_clone_data(x);
182	  }
183	
184	  return x;
185	}
186	```
187	
188	Wrapping a wrapper shares its `data1`, as today. Since `data1` never changes,
189	sharing it between wrappers is safe.
190	
191	`r_clone_data()` reads the ALTREP input with `r_*_cbegin()`, which may
192	allocate inside that ALTREP class. That happens in `r_wrap()`, an ordinary
193	rray C call where allocation is expected.

d3c4869 Wrap ALTREP inputs as is instead of copying them
20bc81f Make the ALTREP wrapper immutable so its data pointers can never dangle

I dropped the copy and pushed it to fix/wrap as d3c4869.

Code: r_wrap() is back to is_wrapper(x) ? wrapper_data(x) : x. It no longer allocates before R_new_altrep(), so the extra KEEP() is gone too, and wrapper_data_from() is removed.

Tests:

  • The ALTREP test in test-wrapper.R now checks that wrap(1:100) shares the compact sequence, and that the wrapper's data is still ALTREP.

  • The mutation battery has two new input types: 1:100 (a compact sequence) and as.character(1:100) (a deferred string). They run the full integer and character case lists, so the battery now also covers the case where the wrapper's data is another class's ALTREP vector.

  • The full suite passes (7,667 tests) and devtools::check() is clean.

Plan: rule 2 now says ALTREP inputs are wrapped as is, and that we rely on each class keeping its data pointer stable, which is the basic contract of Dataptr. It names base R's wrapper as the known exception and calls it base R's bug, fixed in R-devel.

The allocation section now says the wrapper's own methods never allocate. A wrapped ALTREP vector may still allocate on its first data request, the same as reading it directly, so the wrapper adds no new allocation point. I also removed "ALTREP inputs are copied" from the costs.

test_that("a second change happens in place", {
  x <- 1:3
  w <- wrap(x)

  w[1] <- 10L
  address <- obj_address(w)

  w[2] <- 20L
  expect_identical(obj_address(w), address)
  expect_identical(w, c(10L, 20L, 3L))
  expect_identical(x, 1:3)

  x <- as.list(1:3)
  w <- wrap(x)

  w[1] <- list(10L)
  address <- obj_address(w)

  w[2] <- list(20L)
  expect_identical(obj_address(w), address)
  expect_identical(w, list(10L, 20L, 3L))
  expect_identical(x, as.list(1:3))
})

test_that("a temporary wrapper is never reused as an output", {
  x <- c(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6)
  out <- rray_set_dimensions(x, c(2L, 3L)) + 1
  expect_identical(x, c(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6))
  expect_identical(out, array(c(2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7), c(2L, 3L)))

  x <- 1:6
  out <- -rray_set_dimensions(x, c(2L, 3L))
  expect_identical(x, 1:6)
  expect_identical(out, array(-(1:6), c(2L, 3L)))
})

e116229 Use compact sequences again in wrapper tests now that ALTREP inputs are shared

I switched the wrapper tests back to compact sequences (1:3, 1:6, as.raw(1:3) and as.list(1:3)), so they now match the original style. That includes the address checks that share data: they pass again because the wrapper now shares ALTREP inputs instead of copying them. All 2,363 wrapper tests pass, and it's pushed to fix/wrap as e116229.

I left integer = 1:100 + 0L in the mutation battery's helper as it is. The battery needs one plain integer input, and altrep_integer = 1:100 already covers the compact sequence.
